## Delivery model

P00–P10 are agent implementation phases. P00 was added after Run 02 so existing phase IDs and evidence remain stable; it is the required build foundation. R25 adds priority P05.6–P05.8 after Run 03 without renumbering earlier tasks. **M11 is the only phase containing human/manual work**, including product decisions that genuinely require the owner, credentials, physical-device checks, listening review and release acceptance. Agents prepare its artifacts and record evidence; they must not claim human checks have passed.

M11 is one phase with two checkpoints, not separate gates for every implementation phase: prerequisites can be supplied when necessary, and the acceptance pass happens against the integrated candidate. This lets manual work stay together without creating a circular dependency in which a credential needed for implementation is withheld until implementation is finished. Routine engineering choices, automated browser checks, builds, screenshots and fix verification belong in P00–P10.

An implementation phase may be **agent-done** while its linked manual checks remain pending. That permits dependent implementation to continue, but does not make the issue resolved or the release ready. Failed manual checks reopen the owning implementation task; the corresponding retest stays in M11.

### Working assumptions

- Remove Expo completely in P00: use React Native with repository-owned Android/iOS projects, replacement navigation/platform adapters and an independent web build. Preserve local-first storage; do not add accounts, a backend or analytics to deliver this scope.
- Builds must run locally or on self-hosted CI through Gradle and Xcode, without Expo/EAS CLI, accounts, tokens, subscriptions or hosted build quotas. Use Eclipse Timer's local execution/artifact patterns as a reference, but replace its remaining Expo/EAS steps. Platform signing and store access are separate M11 inputs; this requirement does not promise that store distribution has no fees.
- Treat iOS, Android and web as planned targets, because F10 is included. Record actual OS/browser/engine support during implementation; unsupported capability must be explicit.
- Apply the [uniform UI design language](UI_DESIGN_LANGUAGE.md) to any open task that changes a visible flow. Use Reference and Word Converter as the baseline for the bottom tabs, compact shared controls and feedback that keeps nearby content in place.
- Treat NATO tiles as a fixed design composition: preserve the original alphabet's 11:15 portrait ratio, rounded corners, header/body structure and per-letter colors. Share exact geometry and typography across platforms; responsive layout changes columns/outer spacing or uniformly scales the whole tile, never its proportions or individual text styles. R25/P05.6–P05.8 define and verify this contract while preserving large-text accessibility.
- Preserve voice selection. Separate canonical text, pronunciation guides and spoken representation. Start with a replaceable device-speech adapter and honest provider-dependent privacy/offline wording; do not require recordings before useful implementation can proceed. If reviewed recordings are selected, acquisition/rights/listening decisions are tracked in M11.
- Spoken-answer practice is optional to use, but its implementation is in scope. Request microphone access only on explicit use, prefer on-device recognition when available, and do not quietly send audio to a new cloud service. A denied/unavailable engine must leave other practice modes working. A permanently unavailable target needs an owner-approved support decision, not a fake completed feature.
- Web offline support covers an explicitly defined app shell and local learning data. Speech/recognition provider connectivity is a separate capability and must be described accurately.
- Use reasonable reversible defaults. Put genuinely missing owner inputs into M11 and proceed with independent work. Record a changed assumption with its reason and affected tasks. Reprioritization and parked residuals do not imply scope removal or acceptance.
- Publishing, paid services, account setup and release acceptance belong in M11 when required. Prepare a concrete candidate and instructions first; do not invent a need for approval for ordinary local implementation.

## Impact triage and investigation limits

**Current priority amendment — 17 November 2026, after Run 07.** Requested evidence review: [findings and per-run assessment](DELIVERY_PRIORITY_REVIEW.md). This section governs current scheduling when older execution records or task descriptions say all of P05 must finish first. The original portrait design requirement remains; its implemented shared component is the foundation for subsequent features. Reprioritization changes order and blocking scope, not completion, support or release acceptance.

For every new failure, record: owning task; reproducible scenario/platform/settings; actual user consequence; confidence and unrun evidence; workaround/state risk; effort including verification; and the exact flow/task it blocks. Separate **severity**, **scenario reach** and **certainty**. Do not infer prevalence from simulator test counts. Large-text accessibility, lost input/state, incorrect learning/audio, unusable navigation, false privacy claims and reachable security/build failures keep P0/P1 treatment where warranted, even in less common scenarios. A measured raster fringe with readable text and intact geometry can be P3. An unknown cause is not proof of harmlessness.

**Blocking rule:** unfinished work blocks consumers only when the required capability is missing or broken in that consumer. P06/P07 may use delivered P05 controls/tiles while P05 stays active. Their changed-flow readability, concealment, focus, input and audio checks still apply; a reproduced P05.2 failure keeps the affected accessibility acceptance open. Pixel-polish failures alone do not block implementing or validating unrelated features. P10 preparation tasks may also proceed independently; final integrated gates and M11 remain required. Never label a failing check as passing, weaken the font/design contract, suppress native scaling or remove a supported target to obtain progress.

**Investigation boundary:** forecast a concrete diagnostic question, smallest discriminating experiment, expected implementation/verification work and a stopping checkpoint. At that checkpoint, continue only with new evidence supporting a plausible repair and an updated impact/effort forecast. Repeating a falsified hypothesis or broadening matrices without new risk is not progress. Before starting a framework source build, dependency upgrade or new renderer probe, compare its cost with the next useful feature. If no repair is established, park a low-impact issue with a re-entry trigger; retain a genuine P1 as an affected-flow blocker and switch to independent valuable work when practical. Do not spend another whole run on the same inconclusive boundary without an explicit new rationale. This limits investigations, not the number of phases in a run.

**Verification proportional to change:** preserve all existing evidence. Reuse qualified unchanged results, run meaningful checks for affected behavior and capture changed flows on supported targets. Expand the matrix only for a new observed risk or a renderer/font/shared-layout change. Keep stable all-letter/native/window checks historical until relevant changes invalidate them. Diagnostic red results stay red with explicit milestone policy; a nonblocking disposition is not a passed gate. Documentation-only reviews need link/status/source-preservation checks and a screenshot inapplicability record, not new builds or app images.

## Phase overview and dependencies

Effort is a relative forecast input, not a time commitment: S = localized; M = several modules and checks; L = substantial feature/platform integration. Reforecast from the current repository on every execution.

| Phase | Outcome | Dependencies | Forecast | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P00 | Remove Expo; own native projects and independent web/build tooling | Inspect current Run 02 implementation; applicable M11 access only where genuinely needed | L | agent-done |
| P01 | Correct baseline content, truthful claims and passing lint | None | S–M | agent-done |
| P02 | Compatible dependencies, checked application code and CI foundations | P01 | L | agent-done |
| P03 | Reliable preferences, live theming and reset controls | P02 | M | agent-done |
| P04 | Correctly modeled, cancellable audio with reliable voice handling | P02, P03 | L | agent-done |
| P05 | Accessible responsive UI/navigation, exact portrait tile design (R25), and a shared UI design language (P05.9) | P02, P03; use P04 controller when touching audio; P00 native builds for final tile evidence | M–L | active |
| P06 | Consistent converter, copy/share and playback controls | P00/P04 and delivered P05 controls/tiles; affected-flow accessibility checks | M–L | active |
| P07 | Fair, controllable quizzes with sessions and results | P00/P04 and delivered P05 controls/tiles; affected-flow accessibility checks | M | agent-done |
| P08 | Numeric/listening/reverse drills, progress and browsing tools | Per task: canonical data/P04; P07 sessions/results for drills/progress; relevant P06 normalization | L | active |
| P09 | Optional spoken-answer practice with capability/privacy handling | P00/P04, relevant P07/P08 exercise model; support/provider assessment and applicable M11 inputs | L | active |
| P10 | Web offline support, release assets/screenshots, documentation and integrated candidate | Preparation by task; final integrated candidate requires P00–P09 and applicable M11 inputs | M–L | active |
| M11 | Consolidated human prerequisites and final manual acceptance | Prerequisites after relevant preparation; acceptance after P10 | External | todo |

P00–P04 and P07 are agent-done. The shared P05 tile and controls support the delivered P06 converter and P08 practice modes. P06.1 and P08.1/.2/.5 are checked. P06.2/.3/.4, P08.3/.4 and the broader P05 accessibility/fidelity and P10 release gates retain the specific outstanding checks in their task rows. P09.1 assessment is complete and the implementation work remains. The former iOS long-drag failure and the strict iPhone ink gate keep their separate evidence. [Run 32 scope](evidence/run32/README.md).

The amended stages map as follows: **own the build** → P00; **restore trust** → P01/P02; **stabilize core use** → P03–P07; **release confidence** → P00/P02/P05/P10/M11; **learning value** → P06–P09. Tests and documentation grow with each implementation phase rather than being postponed to P10.

## P00 — Remove Expo and own native builds

**Primary:** R24. **Updates:** R08, R14–R16, R20–R22, F10 and the P04 speech adapter. Added 16 November 2026 after Run 02 at the owner's request. Run 03 implements the replacement stack; native acceptance evidence remains distinct from source/bundle verification.

**Reference:** Eclipse Timer's [self-hosted build workflow](../../eclipse-timer/.github/workflows/eas-build.yml) and [runner guide](../../eclipse-timer/documents/planning/self-hosted-macos-runner.md) demonstrate local toolchains and artifact collection. They still use `eas build --local`, Expo packages and an Expo token; its checked-in Android project also invokes Expo tooling, and no iOS project is checked in. Adopt the local ownership pattern, not those dependencies. Merely switching to local EAS or checking in Expo-generated projects does not complete R24.

- [x] **P00.1** Inventory Expo entry/routing, packages and transitive tooling, configuration/plugins, speech, icons/fonts, linking, splash/status/system UI, type generation, lint, scripts and CI. Record replacement choices and a migration sequence that preserves existing screens, URLs/deep links, app identifiers/versioning, preferences, assets and behavior. Keep the working baseline reviewable throughout the migration.
- [x] **P00.2** Replace `expo-router/entry` and Expo Router with non-Expo application/navigation entry points; replace all Expo modules and `@expo/*` runtime/build dependencies, including `expo-speech` and vector icons. Preserve the shared audio controller and its voice/cancellation/lifecycle contract. Move metadata and native settings out of Expo config; remove obsolete plugins, generated-route assumptions and tooling only after their replacements work. Retain React Native and shared app logic; a rewrite in Swift/Kotlin is not required.
- [x] **P00.3** Create and maintain tracked `android/` and `ios/` projects with native resource, permissions, linking and signing configuration. Pin/document compatible Node, Java, Android SDK/Gradle, Xcode and CocoaPods toolchains and lockfiles. Provide direct Gradle debug APK/release AAB commands and Xcode simulator/build/archive/export commands with documented artifact paths. Keep signing material outside the repository; missing private credentials belong in M11.2 and must not block unsigned/debug/simulator work.
- [x] **P00.4** Replace Expo development, dependency-check, lint/type-generation and export commands with documented non-Expo tooling, including an independent React Native Web build and route handling. Update CI for locked install, types/lint/tests, production web/browser checks and direct native builds on suitable local/self-hosted runners. Normal install, development and build paths must not invoke Expo prebuild, Expo CLI, EAS or Expo services.
- [x] **P00.5** Remove obsolete Expo dependencies/configuration from manifests and lockfiles after consumer checks. Inspect the installed/transitive graph and native Gradle/Pod/bundling scripts for residual Expo use. Re-run the security audit and update SECURITY_REVIEW.md and its inventory only from actual new findings; removing Expo does not automatically clear unrelated advisories. Update README, build/runbook and audio docs for the delivered replacements.
- [x] **P00.6** Validate a fresh locked installation, all existing behavioral/type/lint/browser gates, production web build, an installed/running Android debug APK and an installed/running iOS simulator build without Expo tooling or credentials. Exercise routing/back/deep links, theme/storage migration, icons/fonts and speech lifecycle on the new stack; produce release artifacts where signing access exists. Record exact commands, toolchains and artifact identities, then capture available web/Android/iOS screenshots using the execution contract. Reopen affected items on regression.

**Automated exit:** The maintained app, installed dependency graph, web and native build paths no longer depend on Expo/EAS. Direct Android and iOS builds and web checks have actual passing evidence; JS exports alone are insufficient. A missing SDK, simulator or runner is an explicit P00 blocker, not a passed build or a human test. Only credential-dependent signed distribution and physical-device/listening acceptance may remain in M11. Historical Expo evidence remains labeled as such. Release acceptance still requires P10/M11.

**Run 04 completion:** Android SDK installation/licence state is now valid; the debug APK builds, installs and passes real emulator routing/Back, exact input, persisted/legacy preferences and speech lifecycle checks. iOS now uses a single scene, and the native TTS Boolean ABI and Stop readiness/error contracts are repaired. The final iOS 26.2 simulator app passes five runtime tests plus legacy storage migration/reset. Minimal controlled and uncontrolled React Native inputs reproduce the bulk-XCTest failure outside app logic; ordinary consecutive key events, editing, Menu retention and native Paste pass without artificial character sleeps. This satisfies the required resolve-or-isolate investigation; upstream bulk delivery and iOS 27 runtime behavior remain explicit P06/P10 compatibility follow-ups, not claimed fixes or human blockers. [Reports](evidence/run04/README.md), [37 inspected screenshots](evidence/run04/screenshots/index.md).

## P03 — Stabilize preferences and theming

**Primary:** R12 persistence portion, R13. **Starts:** F04.

- [x] **P03.1** Centralize stored preferences with typed defaults and migration from `darkMode`/`selectedVoice`. Define loading/error states and guard against delayed hydration overwriting a newer interaction.
- [x] **P03.2** Implement `system | light | dark`, live OS changes in System mode, consistent content/navigation/system-bar styling, and a stable initial render. Saved-preference restoration must not create a flash or mismatch.
- [x] **P03.3** Make writes robust under rapid changes and failures. Roll back or visibly explain an unsaved value; do not display a saved checkmark for a failed write.
- [x] **P03.4** Add Reset Preferences and System Default voice paths. Reset only the documented settings; distinguish this from later progress deletion. Provide accessible labels/state and update storage documentation.

**Automated exit:** Tests cover first launch, migration, delayed reads, failed writes, rapid toggles, persistence, system changes and reset. Resetting settings does not delete unrelated data. Native appearance checks are M11.4.

## P04 — Deliver reliable audio and voice selection

**Primary:** R03, R04, R05, R09, remaining R12, F04. **Enables:** F03, F06, F08.

- [x] **P04.1** Define separate canonical text, pronunciation guide and speech/asset identifiers; supply intentional pronunciations for all digits, especially `359`. Record the audio strategy and provider limitations. Prepare a full listening script for M11.3.
- [x] **P04.2** Implement one playback owner with a clear replace/queue policy, ordered sequences, cancellation tokens, Stop, token progress and cleanup on navigation/unmount/background. All cards, previews and quiz playback use it.
- [x] **P04.3** Distinguish completed/failed/cancelled outcomes, settle stopped callbacks once, bound missing-callback waits and fall back when storage or saved-voice lookup fails. Cancellation must invalidate queued words and pauses.
- [x] **P04.4** List all eligible English voices grouped/sorted by locale/quality, with Default, loading/empty/error/retry states and removed-voice recovery. Preview and selection must not create unrelated queues or stale saved state.
- [x] **P04.5** Add an audio test/help flow and meaningful failure feedback. Align README, policy and in-app copy with the actual speech provider; do not assert local-only processing without evidence. Avoid logging entered text/audio unnecessarily.

**Automated exit:** Repeated `AB` playback cannot become `Alfa, Alfa, Bravo, Bravo`; Stop/navigation prevent further utterances; storage failure still permits default speech; error/timeout/cancel settle correctly. Mixed-quality/removed voices and selection races are covered. Text-to-audio inputs agree with the model; audible correctness remains pending M11.3.

### P10.8 — Remote build pipeline and reusable host execution

**Run 33 · active.** Trusted branch selection, standalone develop packaging, product inspection, retrieval and controlled-failure reporting are implemented. The hosted execution record identifies each tested revision and residual. [Current commands](RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md#branch-builds-and-downloads) · [Run 33 evidence](evidence/run33/README.md). Earlier implementation checkpoints below describe their dated baseline.

**Priority/sequence:** P2 build/release foundation (R14/R20/R24; P10.4/.5 consumers), active/partial after Run22. M–L effort including runner/platform qualification, shared execution and artifact retrieval. P1 Android readability and compatible security repair remain ahead; pick up the minimum enabling slice sooner if an actual local resource limit prevents their validation and a suitable remote host exists. P3 raster polish does not precede this work. The reported11GiB local capacity is permission to proceed with measured preflight, not proof that a first framework build will fit. This later task does not defer all ongoing engineering.

**Run22 implementation checkpoint:** shared checks/web/native invocation, resource/toolchain/source preflight, one-checkout lock, exact dirty-source transfer inputs, expected-source/drift guards, logs/exit/signal/artifact identities and serial independent unsigned workflow are implemented and locally qualified. Actual remote outputs/retrieval/second invocation and applicable justified framework variant remain unperformed. [Reports/failures](evidence/run22/README.md). No source-framework diagnostic candidate is established; no remote/security/signing/release pass.

**Current workflow baseline.** The branch workflows supersede the Run 33 unsigned artifact matrix. The [develop workflow](../.github/workflows/develop-apk-release.yml) publishes the standalone APK from `develop`. The [mobile release workflow](../.github/workflows/mobile-release.yml) prepares signed products under the owner-approved production environment on `main`. The first signed production run remains pending. [Web CI](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) runs on pull requests and manual dispatch. The historical unsigned simulator workflow is disabled on GitHub. The local unsigned iOS command remains available on an Xcode host. [Published develop APK evidence](evidence/2026-11-20-release-layout.md) · [Runbook](RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md#branch-builds-and-downloads).

**Implementation scope:**

1. Inventory existing jobs/commands and create or refine shared, version-controlled build entry points callable both by GitHub Actions and on another authorized host. Keep host dispatch/transport separate; do not maintain two copies of build logic. Support explicit platform/mode selection, pinned toolchain/locked install, output paths, meaningful exit codes, disk/memory/tool availability preflight and concurrency limits. iOS uses a suitable macOS/Xcode host; Android/web may use independently qualified compatible hosts. Existing Apple-silicon/Ruby-lock support is the starting point, not an unverified Intel/Linux native claim.
2. Build Android Debug APK/Release AAB, unsigned iOS simulator `.app` and the verified static web archive through the shared commands, reusing existing browser/packaging jobs. Prepare signing-capable archive/export modes separately where authorized credentials exist; their absence must not block unsigned builds. Include a selectable RN0.81.5 Android source/framework diagnostic build only after P05.2 establishes a justified candidate/regression and pinned source/ABI/CMake/NDK inputs. Do not silently upgrade/fork the framework or force every routine build to compile it.
3. Separate job results/artifact collection so a failed audit stays failed and blocks release acceptance, while explicitly selected unsigned diagnostic/build jobs can still produce evidence without a success dependency on that audit. Preserve all real compiler/test failures. Restrict private runners to trusted refs/authorized dispatch, keep secrets out of code/logs and keep publishing/store submission behind the existing separate M11 authority. Do not copy Eclipse Timer's automatic submit behavior.
4. Return versioned logs, test results, toolchain inventory, artifact SHA256/source revision and workflow-run URL or equivalent remote job ID. For an intentional dirty candidate, record and transfer the exact reviewed patch/source identity rather than building stale committed code. Retrieve/install the matching artifact for affected native regression and screenshot checks; remote compilation alone does not prove a runtime fix. Record cleanup/cache retention and rerun instructions without removing durable evidence.
5. Document host setup/labels, registration or approved transport, shared invocation, dispatch and artifact retrieval/retry. Validate an actual selected candidate on the remote host, then verify the same shared entry points outside the Actions wrapper (a second invocation on that host is sufficient). Exercise a controlled build failure to verify nonzero job status plus retained logs. No account/paid service/remote host is selected or provisioned by this planning amendment.

#### Branch-triggered app builds

**Release layout update, 20 November 2026.** The owner chose the Eclipse Timer download layout for P10.8. Develop publishes one verified standalone APK as a GitHub prerelease. The approved workflow on `main` publishes the signed Android APK, AAB and iOS IPA together. The earlier matrix below records the Run 33 qualification path. Production signing and acceptance remain M11.7. [Current runbook](RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md#branch-builds-and-downloads) and [first published APK and verification](evidence/2026-11-20-release-layout.md).

**20 November 2026 · Selected for the next pipeline batch.** Use Eclipse Timer's automatic `develop` APK and `main` mobile build as the workflow model. Extend NATO's existing native workflow and shared entry points. Carry over branch triggers, manual reruns, concurrency, toolchain setup and traceable artifact uploads. Keep the current Gradle, Xcode and web commands. [Eclipse Timer dev APK](../../eclipse-timer/.github/workflows/develop-apk-release.yml), [main mobile build](../../eclipse-timer/.github/workflows/eas-build.yml), [runner guide](../../eclipse-timer/documents/planning/self-hosted-macos-runner.md).

| Trigger | Build and retain | Qualification |
|---|---|---|
| Push to `develop` | A development APK with bundled JavaScript, plus the artifact manifest and logs | Use a stable development signing identity for upgrade testing. Verify signature, version and package ID, then install and cold-launch with Metro stopped. Decide and document how the dev build coexists with production and protects existing app data. |
| Push to `main` | Standard Android Release APK and AAB, plus an unsigned iOS simulator app archive. Reuse the existing packaged-web artifact and browser checks from CI. | Verify the configured Android ABIs, bundled JS, fonts and signature status. Inspect the iOS archive and launch the matching app on a supported simulator. Release APK/AAB reports must identify whether signing was available. |
| Trusted manual dispatch on `main` or `develop` | Preserve `all`, `android-debug`, `android-release` and `ios-simulator`. Expose the development APK for targeted builds and reruns. | Verify each selection and its expected output. Run unsigned builds without distribution credentials. Keep signed iOS archive/export and any release-signing inputs in a separately authorized job. |

- **Build entry points.** `android-debug` currently expects Metro. `android-release` currently calls `:app:bundleRelease` and the host wrapper collects only the AAB. Add explicit Gradle tasks and paths for the new APK outputs, preserving the current modes and renderer checks. Use the ordinary source-backed React Native build established in Runs 31–32. A separate framework diagnostic mode still needs a specific P05.2 case. [Direct native script](../scripts/build-native.cjs), [shared host script](../scripts/host-build.cjs), [Run 32](evidence/run32/README.md).
- **Runner and scheduling.** Keep fork PRs on hosted CI. Restrict private-runner jobs to trusted `main`/`develop` pushes and authorized dispatches. Check existing runner availability and toolchain capacity. Use the macOS/Xcode runner for iOS and qualify any independent Android host before using it. Serialize native jobs sharing a host, give attempts distinct output paths and cancel superseded branch builds where safe. Keep docs-only changes from scheduling native work without filtering out build inputs such as lockfiles and shared code.
- **Artifacts and publication.** Upload successful outputs with version, commit/source ID, run ID and attempt, mode, platform/ABI, signing status and SHA-256. Upload failure reports and logs even when a build or check fails. The default destination is GitHub Actions artifacts. Eclipse Timer's GitHub prerelease is a separate, opt-in step for NATO. If approved under M11.7, gate it on a verified installable APK and passing release checks, grant release write permission only to that job, and target the exact tested commit. Store submission remains a separately authorized action.
- **Acceptance.** Exercise the branch and dispatch selection, collect actual hosted or authorized remote run links and download and verify the artifacts. Confirm a controlled failure stays failed and retains logs without publishing a stale artifact. Run the same shared command directly on the remote host and verify source reconstruction when using a reviewed patch. Use scoped launch evidence and screenshots for any changed app flow. Record unavailable runners or signing, and keep remote qualification open until the corresponding jobs run. [Pipeline execution prompt](PIPELINE_EXECUTION_PROMPT.md).

**Owner:** Product/repository owner plus designated device/content reviewers. **Agent role:** Prepare artifacts, instructions, evidence records, fixes and result reconciliation. This is the sole location for human tasks. No phase above should acquire its own separate manual checklist.

### Checkpoint A: prerequisites, only when genuinely required

Run this checkpoint after the agent has prepared a concrete proposal and cannot resolve the missing input from the repository or an authorized reversible default. Batch related inputs in one request; continue independent agent tasks. Do not wait for the final candidate to request a prerequisite that blocks producing it.

- [ ] **M11.1 — Required owner inputs.** Supply/confirm missing support destinations, application ownership/identifiers, chosen target/support exceptions, any recording asset provenance/rights, and recognition-provider choices that require owner action. No response is not approval. Record each input, affected task and actual decision; don't remove F08/F10 merely because their implementation is harder.
- [ ] **M11.2 — Restricted environment access.** Provide signing/developer/hosting access through secure mechanisms when needed, configure physical test devices and any required OS voices/recognition packs. The agent should automate commands/builds once authorized access exists. Record access availability, not secret values. Approved scope changes require a dated reason and remain distinguishable from delivered features. For P10.8, supply access to a suitable chosen remote host and repository runner enrollment/approved transport only when actual remote validation requires it; preparation remains agent work. No remote host/access is assumed available and no new input is needed merely to continue local work.

### Checkpoint B: one integrated manual acceptance pass

Prerequisite: a named candidate revision and relevant installable artifacts from P10. All results must record platform/device, OS/browser, voice/provider where relevant, date, tester, candidate and evidence. Use pass/fail/blocked/not-applicable with a reason; empty checkboxes mean unperformed.

- [ ] **M11.3 — Content and audible pronunciation.** Review all 26 letters and ten digits, canonical spellings and stress; listen to `359` individually and in phrases on supported audio paths. Verify voice preview, rate, pauses and playback highlighting. Review recording rights/provenance if assets are used. Links: R02/R03, F03/F05/F06.
- [ ] **M11.4 — Physical accessibility, appearance and layout.** Complete core flows with VoiceOver and TalkBack, larger native text, reduced motion, small phones, tablets, insets/gesture bars and keyboard-visible input. Verify menu/switch/voice semantics, hidden quiz answer, feedback announcements, focus, live theme changes and saved-theme startup. Cold-start the native Menu deep link and verify that Reference is reachable through the bottom bar and Android Back works after in-app navigation. Inspect themed/cropped launcher icons. Compare all tile contexts against the P05.6 reference for portrait ratio, corners, letter/word/pronunciation placement and typography across device sizes/orientations; verify proportional enlargement and readable, reachable content at larger text settings. Links: R06/R07/R13/R18/R19/R22/R25.
- [ ] **M11.5 — Device workflows and resilience.** Exercise cold starts, rapid Play/select/answer actions, Stop, edits during playback, app background/foreground, calls/interruption, silent-output recovery, missing/removed voices, clipboard/share, sessions/results/mistake retry, progress restoration/deletion and reset. Links: R04/R09/R10/R12/R17, F01–F04/F07/F09.
- [ ] **M11.6 — Microphone, provider privacy and offline behavior.** Exercise spoken-answer mode with permission grant/deny/revoke, unavailable recognition, silence, failures and interruptions. Verify capture stops. Test airplane-mode cold launch and audio/recognition flows, and inspect provider/network behavior against the policy with appropriate tooling. Shell availability alone does not prove offline speech. Review actual data/permission disclosures and resolve any unsupported compliance claims. Links: R05, F08/F10.
- [ ] **M11.7 — Distribution and release acceptance.** Install signed internal iOS/Android candidates produced by the direct native build workflow; verify versions, branding and fresh/upgrade paths. Validate the actual hosted URL/direct routes/cache updates when web hosting is authorized. Review store/support/policy metadata, final cross-platform screenshots and accessibility/content findings, make any applicable owner policy/compliance decisions, and record an explicit release disposition. Publication/store submission occurs only when authorized and supported by the completed candidate, not merely because this checklist exists. Links: R08/R14/R20/R21/R22/R24, F10.

## Execution contract for every reusable-prompt run

### Resume and forecast

1. Find the app root whether the workspace is `apps/` or `nato-phonetics/`. Read applicable instructions, this plan, the review's current delivery section and relevant source. Inspect the working tree; do not discard user/uncommitted changes or repeat completed work blindly.
2. Reconcile recorded state with actual code/evidence. Apply the latest impact triage and task-level dependencies; partial work is not automatically the highest-value next work. Resume it first when user consequence and verification cost justify it. A stale checkbox is not proof of completion. Record discrepancies and repair the ledger.
3. Forecast the next dependency-ready work using scope, touched modules, uncertainty, integration risk, available tools, expected test/build duration and the available execution/context budget. Include time for validation and documentation; do not invent a numeric budget the environment does not expose.
4. Choose a coherent batch that can be finished and verified. **Do not fix a number of phases and do not default to one.** Complete multiple phases when the forecast supports it; a large phase may need more than one run. State the selected IDs and rationale, then implement rather than stopping at the forecast.
5. Reforecast after each completed phase or investigation checkpoint. Triage new failures by impact/confidence before selecting them; record parked residuals and re-entry triggers in the engineering queue. A new strict diagnostic failure does not automatically outrank eligible core features. Continue into further eligible phases when practical, without asking for confirmation at routine phase boundaries. Parallelize independent work when useful. Keep implementation, tests and evidence together.

### Execute, verify and stop honestly

- Implement the chosen work, resolve blocking failures and run the checks appropriate to the change. Keep nonblocking failures visibly open with evidence, priority and re-entry triggers; do not broaden an unrelated gate or call it passed. Apply the investigation boundary before another framework/raster cycle. After available gates pass, do not repeat broad tests without a new change/failure/unresolved concern.
- M11 is the sole human-task queue. Prepare the exact missing input, candidate or test instructions before requesting owner action. Continue independent work while inputs are pending; never treat elapsed time or silence as approval.
- Stop at a coherent boundary when no eligible work remains, a real constraint prevents progress, or the remaining execution budget is needed for verification and a reliable handoff. Do not stop solely because a preset phase count was reached.
- If a selected phase cannot finish, leave it `active` or `blocked` with its precise completed tasks, failing/unrun checks, cause and next action. Do not substitute documentation, a placeholder or mocks for required implemented behavior.
- Do not publish or introduce a paid/cloud dependency as an incidental implementation choice. Honor existing authorization; ask only for genuinely missing authority/input at a concrete M11 checkpoint.

### Screenshot evidence before the final summary

- After completing the batch's implementation and verification, capture screenshots of the running result while preparing the final summary. Cover changed user-facing flows each batch; P10.7 supplies the complete candidate set for **web, Android and iOS**, plus any additional supported target. Documentation-only batches can record captures as not applicable.
- Use the production web build in a browser at desktop and mobile widths, an installed native Android app on an emulator/device, and an installed native iOS app on a simulator/device. A responsive browser image is web evidence only. Use browser automation, `adb exec-out screencap -p`, or `xcrun simctl io <device> screenshot` as appropriate. Eclipse Timer's [Android](../../eclipse-timer/.github/workflows/android-emulator-screenshots.yml) and [iOS](../../eclipse-timer/.github/workflows/ios-simulator-screenshots.yml) capture workflows are references; use P00 builds instead of their EAS steps.
- For the final candidate, show Reference with Letters and Digits, a populated Word Converter, quiz question, feedback and results, Menu, Settings, Voice Select and any newly implemented practice modes. Capture the bottom-tab selection and representative idle and active playback states at matching scroll positions. Include representative light/dark, narrow-screen and important empty/error/permission states using non-sensitive sample content. Capture visible application states, not splash screens or build logs alone.
- Save images under `docs/evidence/<run>/screenshots/<platform>/` (or a durable linked artifact location) with an index recording candidate/build identity, date, platform, OS/browser, device/viewport, theme, screen/state and capture command. Inspect each image for blank/loading screens, clipping and unexpected errors; fix regressions and recapture affected images after any change. Do not fabricate or AI-generate application screenshots.
- Link the index and representative screenshots in the execution record and final summary. For any missing capture, state the target, exact blocker and follow-up owner/task; keep P10.7 incomplete until required captures exist or an explicit owner scope exception is recorded. Missing SDK/runtime/browser tools are agent/environment work; only genuinely restricted access or human acceptance belongs in M11. Screenshots supplement behavioral checks and never prove audible or physical-device acceptance.

### Status meanings and required document updates

Agent phase status: `todo`, `active`, `blocked`, `agent-done`. Use `agent-done` only when implementation tasks and required automated acceptance pass; explicit M11 checks may remain pending. M11 uses `todo`, `active`, `blocked`, `done`.

Item status: `open`, `in-progress`, `implemented-awaiting-M11`, `resolved`, `blocked`, or `scope-change-approved`. `resolved` requires all applicable automated and manual evidence. `scope-change-approved` records an owner decision and is never counted as implemented. Partial task checkboxes do not close the whole item.

After each phase/checkpoint, and before every final response:

1. Update this plan's phase/task status, resolution ledger, M11 queue, decisions and execution record. Record dates, revision/artifact identity (or precise uncommitted diff identity), commands/results, failures/unrun checks and a concrete next action. Never invent commit IDs, test output, human results or approvals.
2. Update APP_REVIEW.md's **Current delivery status** section with status counts, changed R/F IDs, supporting evidence links and remaining risks. Preserve its original review date, snapshot and findings; append resolution evidence instead of rewriting history as though fixes existed at review time.
3. Update README, PRIVACY, help/build/runbook documentation whenever behavior, commands, dependencies, permissions, storage or supported platforms change. Keep the reusable prompt synchronized only if document names or execution rules change.
4. End with a concise summary: phases attempted/completed and why this batch size was chosen; user-visible changes; tests passed/failed/unrun; updated documents; screenshot index/representative images and missing-platform captures with reasons; pending M11 actions/blockers; next eligible work and the reason for stopping. Separate implementation complete from release accepted.

### Execution record

Append one entry per run; keep historical entries. Detailed test logs may live in a linked repository evidence file when useful, but a temporary local path alone is not durable acceptance evidence.

For each implementation run, include:

```text
Run/date and candidate identity:
Forecast: eligible phases, effort/uncertainty, selected batch and rationale.
Delivered: phase/task IDs, R/F IDs, relevant files and user-visible behavior.
Verification: exact commands, results, evidence and unrun checks with reasons.
Screenshots: index/images by platform, candidate identity and missing captures with reasons.
Decisions/deviations: reason, affected scope, owner approval only if actually supplied.
M11: prepared inputs/checks, observed results, failures and retest links.
Handoff: partial work, next eligible task, blockers and reason for stopping.
```

## Definition of overall delivery

All 35 review items have a disposition with evidence; P00–P10 are agent-done, including Expo removal and direct native/web builds; final web/Android/iOS screenshots and their evidence index are linked; applicable M11 acceptance has passed; documents describe the delivered behavior; remaining accepted exceptions are explicit. A scope reduction is reported as a reduction, not delivery of the omitted feature. “Ready for release,” “published” and “implementation complete” are separate statements and require their own evidence.


### Run 01 — 16 November 2026

Candidate: uncommitted work on `main`, base `784bb93c5cfb6edfd318d0dcec2d54d5bb222483`. Initial working tree contained only user-provided untracked `docs/`; no tracked application edits. No applicable AGENTS.md was found in the app or its ancestor directories. Preserve the plan, prompt and historical review.

Forecast: P01 S–M, low integration risk, locked installation/lint/data/export gates expected to take minutes. P02 L, dependency and active-code type uncertainty, now investigated in parallel; it is the intended next phase after P01 passes. P03 M remains a candidate only after the P02 reforecast. No fixed phase count or numeric execution budget assumed. Native listening/appearance/provider validation belongs to M11; none prevents these source changes.

Baseline: host Node 25.6.1 / npm 11.9.0; lint failed with 8 errors/4 warnings; existing `tsc --noEmit` passed but excluded active JS. Baseline web export passed with 11 routes including the placeholder. Initial dependency audit has 49 entries (2 critical/21 high/24 moderate/2 low); SDK54 compatibility check fails. These are current observations, not inherited historical passes. A project-specific Node22 toolchain is being tested before pinning CI.

Selected: P01.1–P01.5, then dependency-ready P02 subject to verification/reforecast. Source fixes remove only dead entry/placeholder route, correct canonical text and misleading copy. F08 remains required in P09. Docs distinguish local app code from provider speech and remove blanket legal guarantees. No approval or owner input has been invented.

P01 checkpoint: `npm ci --no-audit --no-fund` passed (1,004 packages; lifecycle scripts enabled), `CI=1 npm run lint` passed with no findings, `npm test` passed 2/2, and `CI=1 EXPO_NO_TELEMETRY=1 npx --no-install expo export --platform web --output-dir /tmp/nato-p01-web` passed with 10 routes and no voice-quiz route. All under Node22.23.0/npm10.9.4 installed separately in `/tmp/nato-phonetics-toolchain`; .nvmrc/engines/packageManager record the tested baseline. Node's module-type warning will be removed in P02. Native/browser interaction checks not run or claimed.

Reforecast after P01: proceed with P02 (L). SDK54 patch updates are compatible candidates; forced audit fixes are rejected because they can cross SDK families. Checked JS/JSDoc plus pure boundaries preserve file paths and current behavior, reducing migration risk. Clean CI route generation and deliberate-invalid-code probes are needed to establish a meaningful type gate. P03 waits for this evidence; remaining manual checks are all in M11 and do not block P02.

P02 verification checkpoint: clean `npm ci --no-audit --no-fund` passed with 930 installed packages. Broadening lint to scripts initially exposed missing CommonJS `__dirname` globals; targeted ESLint configuration fixed that, and lint then passed with zero findings. Active JS/JSDoc typechecking passed, including 16 maintained JS modules plus shared types and generated routes. Temporary invalid route/data/API fields failed with TS2345/TS2353/TS2322 respectively; all probes were removed and the type gate passed again. `npm test` first passed 4 content/boundary cases; six new audit-review tests also passed. `expo install --check` passed. `expo export --platform all --output-dir /tmp/nato-p02-all` passed (ten web routes, iOS and Android Hermes bundles); this is JS bundling, not native compilation or signed-device validation. A Sharp SVG/PNG/create smoke passed using buffers without changing tracked assets.

Security checkpoint: `npm update` within manifest ranges plus `expo install --fix` aligned SDK54; no forced audit fix or incompatible overrides. Expo54.0.38, Router6.0.24, React Native0.81.5. Raw audit still exits 1 with 26 entries (5 high,20 moderate,1 low,0 critical), reduced from 49. The exact high-entry disposition is in `docs/security-audit-inventory.json`; the live `npm run audit:review` gate passed without hiding raw failure. Owner/remediation/review dates are recorded for every high entry, with 16 December 2026 re-review deadlines. The detailed [security report](SECURITY_REVIEW.md) and machine inventory are complete; seven audit-gate tests and independent review pass.

Browser checkpoint: isolated headless Edge 157.0.4341.60 + Puppeteer24.43.1 against P02 export rendered Home/alphabet/converter/quiz/settings/voice selection. Home and quiz still produced React #418; titles remain empty. These are reproduced R16/R21 failures, not regression passes, and remain P05 work. No signed native builds, speech listening or physical accessibility checks were performed.

P02 exit and reforecast: all P02 tasks and automated gates passed; security inventory and independent gate review cover every residual high entry. The advisory dispositions are not fixes or owner release approval. Proceed into P03 (M) without new packages: one serialized preference store, checked schema migration, deterministic loading boundary, System/Light/Dark choices, scoped reset and saved-selection handling. Pure race/error tests are inexpensive; browser cold-start/reload/live-system checks need a new export. Native system-bar/assistive-technology acceptance remains M11. P04/P05 are the next larger integration boundary and will be reforecast after P03 verification.


#### P03 exit and final reforecast

P03.1–P03.4 are agent-done. `src/lib/preferences.js` owns the versioned `nato.preferences.v1` schema, legacy migration, serial read/write/reset queue and typed error/saved states; `preferenceStore.js` shares the confirmed values with context and speech. Settings offers System/Light/Dark and Reset Preferences; voice selection offers System Default. Root/navigation/native appearance/status-bar integration uses the same theme. README and PRIVACY document migration, save errors, exact reset scope and provider limitations. No raw speech text is logged. Native rendering and audible output remain unverified.

`npm test` passed **26/26**: 2 canonical, 2 pure conversion/question boundaries, 7 audit-review, 15 preference tests. Preference coverage includes first launch, both legacy themes, voice migration, delayed read/early change, invalid schema, read/migration/write/cleanup/reset failures, serial rapid changes, persistence/restart, scoped deletion, deterministic server snapshot and live theme resolution. Lint and active-code typecheck passed after the final UI fix. Independent reviews found no remaining blocker in the delivered store or security gate.

The first browser run failed: the theme and storage changed, but React Native Web0.21 omitted `accessibilityState.checked` from the DOM. A diagnostic rerun confirmed the same failure. The fix adds explicit `aria-checked` alongside native state on theme/voice radios; assertions were retained. Final browser check exited **0** using Edge 157.0.4341.60/Puppeteer24.43.1, verifying first launch, saved appearance reload, explicit override, return to System, live OS changes, legacy migration, System Default, scoped reset preserving an unrelated progress key, and failed writes retaining saved selection with a visible alert. Direct Home/Settings/Voice flows had **no page/hydration errors**. This is a bounded P03 check; it does not close R16's full P05 matrix or physical accessibility acceptance.

Final export command: `CI=1 EXPO_NO_TELEMETRY=1 npx --no-install expo export --platform all --output-dir /tmp/nato-run01-final` passed with **10 web routes plus iOS/Android Hermes bundles**. **Review repairs and limitations:** Native TTS4.1.1 Android identified utterances by spoken-text hash. The version-guarded install patch changes this to UUIDs; otherwise rapid identical words could receive each other's delayed terminal events. Adapter tests cover initialization/Stop races, early events, correlation, cleanup/default reset/rate mapping and failures. iOS supports no tts-error event, so it subscribes only to declared events and relies on promise failures/timeouts. Actual voice-quality wire values and native rate conversion were corrected and tested. Native audible correctness remains M11 listening work; mocked lifecycle checks do not prove it. Xcode27 rejected the inherited AsyncStorage resource target minimum and fmt11.0.2 consteval path; the Podfile explicitly raises dependency target floors to15.1 and applies a version/source-guarded Apple-compiler fallback. Patch idempotence/source drift/version drift and actual compiler macro checks pass ([evidence](evidence/run03-fmt-patch-checks.txt)); the resulting native binary built successfully. Browser navigation now retains keyed history entries and releases audio on blur; Menu/Back preserves converter/quiz state. Web is intentionally client-rendered with a stable neutral shell; offline caching remains P10.
